Great question! Building a real-world pharmacy/medicine e-commerce website is a solid project. Let me give you a complete, professional roadmap.

Here's a complete, professional roadmap for building your pharmacy/medicine e-commerce website from scratch.



Here's the full breakdown of each phase. You can click any phase above to dive deeper.

Phase 1 — Planning & Setup

Start by deciding your tech stack. For a real-world pharmacy site in Bangladesh, a good modern choice is: Next.js (React framework) for the frontend, Node.js + Express or Next.js API routes for the backend, MongoDB or PostgreSQL for the database, and Cloudinary for storing prescription images. For mobile-first Bangladesh users, make sure the UI is responsive from day one.

Phase 2 — Database Design

Your core tables/collections will be: Users (customers + admins), Products (medicines, syrups, etc. with price, stock, requires_prescription flag), Orders, OrderItems, Prescriptions (linked to a user and optionally an order), and Notifications. The requires_prescription flag on products is critical — it gates checkout for controlled items.

Phase 3 — Authentication & Roles

Use JWT tokens or a library like NextAuth.js. You need two roles: Customer and Admin. Customers can register, log in, manage their profile, upload prescriptions, and track orders. Admins can manage products, review prescriptions, update order statuses, and see analytics. Never let a customer access admin routes.

Phase 4 — Product Catalog

Build pages for: product listing with search + filter (by category, price, brand), individual product detail page, related products, and an out-of-stock indicator. For medicines, include: generic name, brand name, dosage, usage instructions, and a "Prescription Required" badge where needed.

Phase 5 — Prescription Upload System (Your Key Feature)

This is what makes your site stand out. The flow works like this:



When a customer tries to buy a prescription-only item, they are prompted to upload their prescription (image/PDF). The admin sees a queue of pending prescriptions in the dashboard, can view the image, then approve or reject it with a note. The customer gets notified. Only approved prescriptions unlock checkout for that item.

Phase 6 — Cart, Checkout & Payments

Standard e-commerce cart with quantity controls. At checkout, block any prescription-required items that haven't been approved yet. For Bangladesh, integrate bKash, Nagad, or SSL Commerz as payment gateways. Also offer cash-on-delivery as an option since it's very popular locally.

Phase 7 — Admin Dashboard

The admin panel needs: a prescription review queue (most urgent), order management with status updates (Pending → Processing → Shipped → Delivered), product/inventory management (add, edit, update stock), a customer list, and a simple sales analytics page (daily revenue, top products).

Phase 8 — Notifications

Use Nodemailer for email and Twilio or a local SMS provider for SMS. Key notifications to build: order confirmed, prescription approved/rejected, order shipped (with tracking info), and low-stock alerts to the admin.

Phase 9 — Testing & Deployment

Test every prescription flow manually. For deployment: Vercel (easiest for Next.js frontend), Railway or Render for the backend, MongoDB Atlas for the database. Get an SSL certificate (free via Let's Encrypt). Before going live, have a pharmacist or doctor review that your prescription handling complies with Bangladesh drug regulations.

Phase 10 — Ongoing

After launch: collect customer feedback, monitor for bugs, add features like order history, wishlists, and loyalty points. Run occasional discounts during Eid or health awareness months.

Recommended Tech Stack Summary

Layer	Technology
Frontend	Next.js (React)
Backend	Next.js API routes or Express.js
Database	MongoDB Atlas or PostgreSQL
File Storage	Cloudinary (prescriptions/images)
Payments	bKash / SSLCommerz / Nagad
Email	Nodemailer + Gmail or SendGrid
Hosting	Vercel + Railway
Auth	NextAuth.js or JWT
Would you like me to go deep on any specific phase — for example, write the actual code for the prescription upload system, the database schema, or the admin dashboard?

Mar 27
Claude responded: The schema is large, so I'll split it into three focused ERDs — User & Auth, Product & Inventory, and Orders & Prescriptions — so each one stays readable.
The schema is large, so I'll split it into three focused ERDs — User & Auth, Product & Inventory, and Orders & Prescriptions — so each one stays readable.

Part 1: User & Auth domain — the foundation everything else connects to.



Part 2: Product & Inventory domain — the catalog, batches, stock, categories, brands, reviews, and wishlists.



Part 3: Orders & Prescriptions domain — the core transaction and pharmacy-specific workflow tables.



Here's a quick summary of how the three domains connect:

USER is the anchor — it connects to ADDRESS, ORDER, CART, PRESCRIPTION, REVIEW, WISHLIST_ITEM, and NOTIFICATION via user_id
PRODUCT sits at the center of the catalog domain and is referenced by ORDER_ITEM, CART_ITEM, PRESCRIPTION_ITEM, REVIEW, and WISHLIST_ITEM
PRESCRIPTION → PRESCRIPTION_ITEM → ORDER_ITEM is the critical pharmacy-specific chain — an ORDER_ITEM can optionally reference a prescription_id to prove the medicine was approved before purchase
INVENTORY_BATCH gives you per-batch expiry tracking on each product, separate from the main stock count
reviewed_by on PRESCRIPTION is a FK back to USER (with role = admin/pharmacist), giving you the full audit trail of who approved what

Mar 28
Claude responded: Great choice!
Great choice! ASP.NET Core is excellent for a pharmacy e-commerce backend — it's fast, scalable, and production-ready. Let me lay out the full plan first.

What we'll build — ASP.NET Core Backend for MediCare+
Tech stack:

ASP.NET Core 8 Web API
Entity Framework Core (ORM)
PostgreSQL (database)
JWT Authentication
AutoMapper (DTO mapping)
Swagger (API docs)
The full plan — step by step
Step 1 — Project Setup Create the ASP.NET Core Web API project, install all NuGet packages, set up folder structure, configure appsettings.json with database connection string.

Step 2 — Database Models (Entities) Create all C# model classes based on your ER diagram — User, Address, Product, Category, Brand, InventoryBatch, Cart, CartItem, Order, OrderItem, Payment, Prescription, PrescriptionItem, Review, Coupon, Notification, AuditLog, Wishlist.

Step 3 — DbContext & EF Migrations Set up AppDbContext, configure all relationships (one-to-many, many-to-many), run first migration to create the database schema.

Step 4 — DTOs & AutoMapper Create Data Transfer Objects (request/response models) for every entity so we never expose raw database models to the API. Set up AutoMapper profiles.

Step 5 — Authentication & Roles (JWT) Build register, login, and refresh token endpoints. Set up roles: Customer, Admin, Pharmacist. Protect routes with [Authorize] attributes.

Step 6 — Product & Catalog APIs CRUD for products, categories, brands. Search and filter endpoints. Stock management. requires_prescription flag enforcement.

Step 7 — Cart & Wishlist APIs Add to cart, update quantity, remove item, clear cart. Wishlist add/remove.

Step 8 — Order & Checkout APIs Place order, apply coupon, calculate delivery charge, create order items, block checkout if Rx item has no approved prescription.

Step 9 — Prescription Module APIs Upload prescription (file + metadata), admin review endpoints (approve / reject / request clarification), link approved medicines, notify customer.

Step 10 — Payment APIs Create payment record, handle COD and gateway callback, update payment status.

Step 11 — Admin APIs Dashboard stats, user management, inventory management, order management, expiry alerts, low stock alerts.

Step 12 — Notifications In-app notifications system. Email via SMTP (Nodemailer equivalent in .NET = MailKit).

Folder structure we'll use
MediCare.API/
├── Controllers/
├── Models/          ← EF Core entities
├── DTOs/            ← Request & Response models
├── Data/            ← DbContext
├── Repositories/    ← Data access layer
├── Services/        ← Business logic
├── Middleware/      ← Error handling, logging
├── Helpers/         ← JWT, file upload, etc.
├── Migrations/
└── appsettings.json
Order we'll go in
#	Step	What you learn
1	Project setup	.NET CLI, NuGet, project structure
2	Models	EF Core entities, data annotations
3	DbContext + Migration	EF relationships, PostgreSQL setup
4	DTOs + AutoMapper	Clean API design pattern
5	JWT Auth	Token-based auth, roles, middleware
6	Products API	CRUD, search, filter
7	Cart & Wishlist	Session vs DB cart
8	Orders API	Business logic, validation
9	Prescription API	File upload, status workflow
10	Payment API	COD + gateway integration
11	Admin API	Dashboard, reports
12	Notifications	Email + in-app
Ready to start? Just say "Start Step 1" and I'll walk you through the full project setup with every command and every file — explaining what each thing does as we go.

Claude responded: Perfect — you have everything ready!
Perfect — you have everything ready! Let's start Step 1 right now.

Step 1 — Project Setup
We'll do everything using the terminal (Command Prompt or PowerShell). Open it and follow each command one by one. I'll explain what every command does.

1. Create the solution folder
bash
mkdir MediCare
cd MediCare
mkdir MediCare — creates a new folder called MediCare on your machine. cd MediCare — moves you inside that folder.

2. Create the solution file
bash
dotnet new sln -n MediCare
This creates a MediCare.sln file. Think of a solution file as a container that holds all your projects together. Visual Studio uses it to know what belongs to this workspace.

3. Create the Web API project
bash
dotnet new webapi -n MediCare.API --no-openapi
This creates a new ASP.NET Core Web API project inside a folder called MediCare.API. --no-openapi skips the default minimal API setup — we'll add Swagger ourselves properly.

4. Add the project to the solution
bash
dotnet sln add MediCare.API/MediCare.API.csproj
This links your MediCare.API project to the solution file so they know about each other.

5. Go into the project folder
bash
cd MediCare.API
6. Install all NuGet packages we need
Run these one by one:

bash
dotnet add package Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore
EF Core — the ORM that lets us talk to the database using C# classes instead of raw SQL.

bash
dotnet add package Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design
Required for running migrations (creating/updating database tables from code).

bash
dotnet add package Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL
The PostgreSQL driver for EF Core — lets EF Core connect to your PostgreSQL database.

bash
dotnet add package Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer
Adds JWT (JSON Web Token) authentication support — used for login/logout/protected routes.

bash
dotnet add package System.IdentityModel.Tokens.Jwt
The library that creates and validates JWT tokens.

bash
dotnet add package AutoMapper
Maps between your database models and your DTOs automatically — saves a lot of manual copying.

bash
dotnet add package AutoMapper.Extensions.Microsoft.DependencyInjection
Wires AutoMapper into ASP.NET Core's built-in dependency injection system.

bash
dotnet add package Swashbuckle.AspNetCore
Adds Swagger UI — a browser-based page where you can test all your API endpoints visually.

bash
dotnet add package BCrypt.Net-Next
Used to securely hash and verify passwords. Never store plain text passwords.

bash
dotnet add package CloudinaryDotNet
For uploading prescription images/files to Cloudinary cloud storage.

bash
dotnet add package MailKit
For sending emails (order confirmations, prescription approval notifications, etc).

7. Set up the folder structure
Still inside MediCare.API, run these commands to create all the folders:

bash
mkdir Controllers
mkdir Models
mkdir DTOs
mkdir Data
mkdir Repositories
mkdir Services
mkdir Interfaces
mkdir Helpers
mkdir Middleware
mkdir Mappings
Each folder has a specific job:

Controllers — API endpoints (what the frontend calls)
Models — Database entity classes
DTOs — What we send/receive through the API (not raw DB models)
Data — DbContext (database connection and configuration)
Repositories — Database query logic
Services — Business logic (e.g. "can this user buy this medicine?")
Interfaces — Contracts that Repositories and Services must follow
Helpers — JWT generator, file upload helper, etc.
Middleware — Global error handler, request logging
Mappings — AutoMapper profile configuration
8. Configure appsettings.json
Open MediCare.API/appsettings.json and replace everything with this:

json
{
  "ConnectionStrings": {
    "DefaultConnection": "Host=localhost;Port=5432;Database=MediCareDB;Username=postgres;Password=YOUR_POSTGRES_PASSWORD"
  },
  "JwtSettings": {
    "SecretKey": "MediCare_Super_Secret_Key_Change_This_In_Production_2025",
    "Issuer": "MediCare.API",
    "Audience": "MediCare.Client",
    "ExpiryInDays": 7
  },
  "CloudinarySettings": {
    "CloudName": "your_cloud_name",
    "ApiKey": "your_api_key",
    "ApiSecret": "your_api_secret"
  },
  "EmailSettings": {
    "Host": "smtp.gmail.com",
    "Port": 587,
    "Username": "your_email@gmail.com",
    "Password": "your_app_password",
    "FromName": "MediCare+"
  },
  "Logging": {
    "LogLevel": {
      "Default": "Information",
      "Microsoft.AspNetCore": "Warning"
    }
  },
  "AllowedHosts": "*"
}
Replace YOUR_POSTGRES_PASSWORD with your actual PostgreSQL password. Leave Cloudinary and Email as placeholders for now — we'll fill them in when we reach those steps.

9. Clean up default files
Delete these files that were auto-generated and we don't need:

bash
del WeatherForecast.cs
del Controllers\WeatherForecastController.cs
10. Verify everything works
bash
dotnet build
You should see Build succeeded with 0 errors. This confirms all packages installed correctly and the project compiles.

Your folder structure should now look like this:
MediCare/
├── MediCare.sln
└── MediCare.API/
    ├── Controllers/
    ├── Models/
    ├── DTOs/
    ├── Data/
    ├── Repositories/
    ├── Services/
    ├── Interfaces/
    ├── Helpers/
    ├── Middleware/
    ├── Mappings/
    ├── appsettings.json
    ├── Program.cs
    └── MediCare.API.csproj
Step 1 is done ✅
Run all the commands, and when you see Build succeeded — come back and say "Step 1 done" and we'll move straight into Step 2 — Creating all the Database Models (Entities).

If you get any error at any point, just paste the error message here and I'll fix it with you immediately.

Mar 28
Claude responded: Great, Step 1 is done!
Great, Step 1 is done! Now let's write all the Database Models (Entities). These are C# classes that represent your database tables.

Step 2 — Database Models (Entities)
Every file goes inside your Models folder.

Right click Models folder → Add → New Item → Class

1. First — create a Base class
Create file: Models/BaseEntity.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public abstract class BaseEntity
    {
        public int Id { get; set; }
        public DateTime CreatedAt { get; set; } = DateTime.UtcNow;
        public DateTime? UpdatedAt { get; set; }
    }
}
This is a base class — every model will inherit from it. So every table automatically gets Id, CreatedAt, and UpdatedAt columns. No need to repeat these in every model.

2. User
Create file: Models/User.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public class User : BaseEntity
    {
        public string Name { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Email { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Phone { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string PasswordHash { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Role { get; set; } = "Customer"; // Customer, Admin, Pharmacist
        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;

        // Navigation properties
        public ICollection<Address> Addresses { get; set; } = new List<Address>();
        public ICollection<Order> Orders { get; set; } = new List<Order>();
        public ICollection<Prescription> Prescriptions { get; set; } = new List<Prescription>();
        public ICollection<Review> Reviews { get; set; } = new List<Review>();
        public ICollection<Notification> Notifications { get; set; } = new List<Notification>();
        public ICollection<WishlistItem> WishlistItems { get; set; } = new List<WishlistItem>();
        public Cart? Cart { get; set; }
    }
}
Role controls what this user can do — Customer, Admin, or Pharmacist. Navigation properties (like ICollection<Address>) tell EF Core about relationships — one User has many Addresses.

3. Address
Create file: Models/Address.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public class Address : BaseEntity
    {
        public int UserId { get; set; }
        public string FullName { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Phone { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string District { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Area { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string AddressLine { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string PostalCode { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public bool IsDefault { get; set; } = false;

        // Navigation
        public User User { get; set; } = null!;
    }
}
UserId is the foreign key — it links this address to a specific user. User User is the navigation property — lets you do address.User.Name in code.

4. Category
Create file: Models/Category.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public class Category : BaseEntity
    {
        public string Name { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Slug { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string? ImageUrl { get; set; }
        public int? ParentCategoryId { get; set; }
        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;

        // Navigation
        public Category? ParentCategory { get; set; }
        public ICollection<Category> SubCategories { get; set; } = new List<Category>();
        public ICollection<Product> Products { get; set; } = new List<Product>();
    }
}
ParentCategoryId is nullable (int?) — top-level categories have no parent. This supports nested categories like: Medicines → Antibiotics → Syrups.

6. Product
Create file: Models/Product.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public class Product : BaseEntity
    {
        public string Name { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string Slug { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string SKU { get; set; } = string.Empty;
        public string? Description { get; set; }
        public string? ShortDescription { get; set; }

        // Foreign Keys
        public int CategoryId { get; set; }
        public int BrandId { get; set; }

        // Pricing
        public decimal Price { get; set; }
        public decimal? DiscountPrice { get; set; }

        // Stock
        public int Stock { get; set; } = 0;

        // Pharmacy-specific fields
        public bool RequiresPrescription { get; set; } = false;
        public string? DosageForm { get; set; }    // Syrup, Tablet, Capsule, Injection
        public string? Strength { get; set; }       // 500mg, 100ml, 5mg/5ml
        public string? PackSize { get; set; }       // 10 tablets, 100ml bottle
        public string? GenericName { get; set; }
        public string? Manufacturer { get; set; }
        public string? ActiveIngredient { get; set; }
        public string? Indications { get; set; }    // What it's used for
        public string? SideEffects { get; set; }
        public string? Warnings { get; set; }
        public string? StorageInfo { get; set; }
        public string? PregnancyWarning { get; set; }
        public string? ChildSafetyInfo { get; set; }

        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;

        // Navigation
        public Category Category { get; set; } = null!;
        public Brand Brand { get; set; } = null!;
        public ICollection<ProductImage> Images { get; set; } = new List<ProductImage>();
        public ICollection<InventoryBatch> InventoryBatches { get; set; } = new List<InventoryBatch>();
        public ICollection<Review> Reviews { get; set; } = new List<Review>();
        public ICollection<WishlistItem> WishlistItems { get; set; } = new List<WishlistItem>();
        public ICollection<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; } = new List<OrderItem>();
        public ICollection<CartItem> CartItems { get; set; } = new List<CartItem>();
        public ICollection<PrescriptionItem> PrescriptionItems { get; set; } = new List<PrescriptionItem>();
    }
}
This is your most important model — it has all the pharmacy-specific fields from your original requirements like RequiresPrescription, DosageForm, Strength, etc.

11. Order

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    public class Order : BaseEntity
    {
        public int UserId { get; set; }
        public int AddressId { get; set; }
        public int? CouponId { get; set; }

        public decimal Subtotal { get; set; }
        public decimal Discount { get; set; } = 0;
        public decimal DeliveryCharge { get; set; } = 0;
        public decimal Total { get; set; }

        public string PaymentMethod { get; set; } = "COD"; // COD, bKash, Card
        public string PaymentStatus { get; set; } = "Unpaid"; // Unpaid, Paid, Failed, Refunded
        public string OrderStatus { get; set; } = "Pending"; // Pending, Confirmed, Processing, Packed, Shipped, Delivered, Cancelled, Returned

        public string? Note { get; set; }

        // Navigation
        public User User { get; set; } = null!;
        public Address Address { get; set; } = null!;
        public Coupon? Coupon { get; set; }
        public ICollection<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; } = new List<OrderItem>();
        public Payment? Payment { get; set; }
    }
}

        public Prescription? Prescription { get; set; }
    }

PrescriptionId is nullable — only filled in when the ordered item required a prescription.

    {
        public int? UserId { get; set; }           // Who did the action
        public string Action { get; set; } = string.Empty;       // e.g. "Approved Prescription"

AuditLog tracks every important admin action — who approved what, when, from which IP. Very important for a medical platform.

Claude responded: Understood!
Understood! Let me clarify the exact roles before we continue:

Two roles only
👤 Customer (User)
Register / Login
Browse & search products
Add to cart / wishlist
Place orders
Upload prescription
Track orders
View prescription status & admin response
Write reviews
Manage their profile & addresses
🔧 Admin
Everything the customer can do PLUS:
Manage products, categories, brands
Manage stock & inventory batches
Manage all orders (confirm, pack, ship, deliver)
Review prescriptions (approve / reject / request clarification)
Suggest substitute medicines
Manage coupons & offers
Manage users
View reports & analytics
Manage banners
Send notifications
View audit logs
Manage expiry alerts & low stock alerts
No Pharmacist role. Admin handles all prescription review and approval work.

What changes in the code
In User.cs the Role field becomes simply:

csharp
public string Role { get; set; } = "Customer"; // Customer or Admin only
And in Prescription.cs the ReviewedBy will always point to an Admin user.

1. Roles Constant
Create file: Helpers/Constants/Roles.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Defines all user roles in the system.
    /// Only two roles exist — Admin manages everything.
    /// </summary>
    public static class Roles
    {
        public const string Admin    = "Admin";
        public const string Customer = "Customer";
    }
}

9. BaseEntity
Create file: Models/BaseEntity.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Base class inherited by all entities.
    /// Provides common audit fields automatically.
    /// </summary>
    public abstract class BaseEntity
    {
        public int Id { get; set; }
        public DateTime CreatedAt { get; set; } = DateTime.UtcNow;
        public DateTime? UpdatedAt { get; set; }
    }
}
10. User
Create file: Models/User.cs

csharp
using MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants;

namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a registered user.
    /// Role is either "Admin" or "Customer".
    /// Admin has full system access.
    /// </summary>
    public class User : BaseEntity
    {
        public string Name { get; set; } = null!;
        public string Email { get; set; } = null!;
        public string Phone { get; set; } = null!;
        public string PasswordHash { get; set; } = null!;
        public string Role { get; set; } = Roles.Customer;
        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;
        public string? ProfileImageUrl { get; set; }
        public DateTime? LastLoginAt { get; set; }

        // Navigation properties
        public ICollection<Address> Addresses { get; set; }          = new List<Address>();
        public ICollection<Order> Orders { get; set; }               = new List<Order>();
        public ICollection<Prescription> Prescriptions { get; set; } = new List<Prescription>();
        public ICollection<Review> Reviews { get; set; }             = new List<Review>();
        public ICollection<Notification> Notifications { get; set; } = new List<Notification>();
        public ICollection<WishlistItem> WishlistItems { get; set; } = new List<WishlistItem>();
        public ICollection<AuditLog> AuditLogs { get; set; }         = new List<AuditLog>();
        public Cart? Cart { get; set; }
    }
}
11. Address
Create file: Models/Address.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a customer's delivery address.
    /// A user can have multiple addresses with one marked as default.
    /// </summary>
    public class Address : BaseEntity
    {
        public int UserId { get; set; }
        public string FullName { get; set; }    = null!;
        public string Phone { get; set; }       = null!;
        public string District { get; set; }    = null!;
        public string Area { get; set; }        = null!;
        public string AddressLine { get; set; } = null!;
        public string? PostalCode { get; set; }
        public bool IsDefault { get; set; } = false;

        // Navigation
        public User User { get; set; } = null!;
        public ICollection<Order> Orders { get; set; } = new List<Order>();
    }
}
12. Category
Create file: Models/Category.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a product category.
    /// Supports parent-child hierarchy for nested categories.
    /// Example: Medicines → Antibiotics → Syrups
    /// </summary>
    public class Category : BaseEntity
    {
        public string Name { get; set; }  = null!;
        public string Slug { get; set; }  = null!;
        public string? ImageUrl { get; set; }
        public string? Description { get; set; }
        public int? ParentCategoryId { get; set; }
        public int SortOrder { get; set; } = 0;
        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;

        // Navigation
        public Category? ParentCategory { get; set; }
        public ICollection<Category> SubCategories { get; set; } = new List<Category>();
        public ICollection<Product> Products { get; set; }        = new List<Product>();
    }
}

14. Product
Create file: Models/Product.cs

csharp
using MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants;

namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a medicine or health product in the catalog.
    /// Contains all pharmacy-specific fields required for
    /// proper medicine identification and safety information.
    /// </summary>
    public class Product : BaseEntity
    {
        // ── Core fields ──────────────────────────────────
        public string Name { get; set; }             = null!;
        public string Slug { get; set; }             = null!;
        public string SKU { get; set; }              = null!;
        public string? Description { get; set; }
        public string? ShortDescription { get; set; }

        // ── Relations ────────────────────────────────────
        public int CategoryId { get; set; }
        public int BrandId { get; set; }

        // ── Pricing ──────────────────────────────────────
        public decimal Price { get; set; }
        public decimal? DiscountPrice { get; set; }

        // ── Stock ────────────────────────────────────────
        public int Stock { get; set; } = 0;
        public int LowStockThreshold { get; set; } = 10;

        // ── Pharmacy-specific fields ──────────────────────
        public bool RequiresPrescription { get; set; } = false;

        /// <summary>Syrup, Tablet, Capsule, Injection etc.</summary>
        public string? DosageForm { get; set; }

        /// <summary>e.g. 500mg, 100ml, 5mg/5ml</summary>
        public string? Strength { get; set; }

        /// <summary>e.g. 10 tablets, 100ml bottle</summary>
        public string? PackSize { get; set; }

        /// <summary>Generic/chemical name e.g. Paracetamol</summary>
        public string? GenericName { get; set; }

        public string? Manufacturer { get; set; }
        public string? ActiveIngredient { get; set; }
        public string? Indications { get; set; }
        public string? SideEffects { get; set; }
        public string? Warnings { get; set; }
        public string? Contraindications { get; set; }
        public string? StorageInfo { get; set; }
        public string? PregnancyWarning { get; set; }
        public string? ChildSafetyInfo { get; set; }

        // ── Status ───────────────────────────────────────
        public bool IsActive { get; set; } = true;
        public bool IsFeatured { get; set; } = false;

        // ── Navigation ───────────────────────────────────
        public Category Category { get; set; }                             = null!;
        public Brand Brand { get; set; }                                   = null!;
        public ICollection<ProductImage> Images { get; set; }             = new List<ProductImage>();
        public ICollection<InventoryBatch> InventoryBatches { get; set; } = new List<InventoryBatch>();
        public ICollection<Review> Reviews { get; set; }                  = new List<Review>();
        public ICollection<WishlistItem> WishlistItems { get; set; }      = new List<WishlistItem>();
        public ICollection<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; }            = new List<OrderItem>();
        public ICollection<CartItem> CartItems { get; set; }              = new List<CartItem>();
        public ICollection<PrescriptionItem> PrescriptionItems { get; set; } = new List<PrescriptionItem>();
    }
}

19. Order
Create file: Models/Order.cs

csharp
using MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants;

namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a customer order from placement to delivery.
    /// Order status moves through a defined lifecycle managed by Admin.
    /// </summary>
    public class Order : BaseEntity
    {
        public int UserId { get; set; }
        public int AddressId { get; set; }
        public int? CouponId { get; set; }

        // ── Financials ───────────────────────────────────
        public decimal Subtotal { get; set; }
        public decimal Discount { get; set; }       = 0;
        public decimal DeliveryCharge { get; set; } = 0;
        public decimal Total { get; set; }

        // ── Payment ──────────────────────────────────────
        public string PaymentMethod { get; set; }  = PaymentMethods.COD;
        public string PaymentStatus { get; set; }  = PaymentStatuses.Unpaid;

        // ── Status ───────────────────────────────────────
        public string OrderStatus { get; set; }    = OrderStatuses.Pending;

        public string? Note { get; set; }
        public string? CancellationReason { get; set; }

        // ── Navigation ───────────────────────────────────
        public User User { get; set; }                             = null!;
        public Address Address { get; set; }                       = null!;
        public Coupon? Coupon { get; set; }
        public Payment? Payment { get; set; }
        public ICollection<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; }     = new List<OrderItem>();
    }
}

csharp
using MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants;

namespace MediCare.API.Models

    /// <summary>

    /// One payment record per order.

    {

        public string? TransactionId { get; set; }
        public string Method { get; set; }  = PaymentMethods.COD;
        public decimal Amount { get; set; }

        // Navigation

    }

22. Prescription
Create file: Models/Prescription.cs

csharp
using MediCare.API.Helpers.Constants;

namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a prescription uploaded by a customer.
    /// Admin reviews and approves/rejects it.
    /// Approved prescriptions unlock Rx-required medicines for purchase.
    /// </summary>
    public class Prescription : BaseEntity
    {
        public int UserId { get; set; }
        public string PatientName { get; set; }  = null!;
        public string FileUrl { get; set; }      = null!;  // Cloudinary URL
        public string? PublicId { get; set; }              // Cloudinary public ID
        public string? Message { get; set; }               // Customer's note to admin

        // ── Review fields (filled by Admin) ──────────────
        public string Status { get; set; }       = PrescriptionStatuses.Pending;
        public string? AdminNote { get; set; }             // Admin response to customer
        public int? ReviewedBy { get; set; }               // Admin user ID
        public DateTime? ReviewedAt { get; set; }

        // ── Navigation ───────────────────────────────────
        public User User { get; set; }                                         = null!;
        public ICollection<PrescriptionItem> PrescriptionItems { get; set; }   = new List<PrescriptionItem>();
        public ICollection<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; }                 = new List<OrderItem>();
    }
}

28. AuditLog
Create file: Models/AuditLog.cs

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Models
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Immutable log of every significant admin action.
    /// Used for accountability, debugging and compliance.
    /// Never updated — only inserted.
    /// </summary>
    public class AuditLog : BaseEntity
    {
        public int? UserId { get; set; }
        public string Action { get; set; }     = null!;  // e.g. "ApprovedPrescription"
        public string ObjectType { get; set; } = null!;  // e.g. "Prescription"
        public int? ObjectId { get; set; }
        public string? OldValues { get; set; }           // JSON snapshot before change
        public string? NewValues { get; set; }           // JSON snapshot after change
        public string? IpAddress { get; set; }
        public string? UserAgent { get; set; }

        // Navigation
        public User? User { get; set; }
    }
}

i want to push it in github repo tell me the production level way to push it bevause it is a real life project

Mar 29
Claude responded: Great thinking!
Great thinking! For a real-life project, you never just push everything to GitHub carelessly. Here's the professional way to do it.

Before pushing — Production GitHub Setup
Step 1 — Create a .gitignore file first
This is the most important step. You must never push secrets, build files, or local config to GitHub.

In Visual Studio: Right click solution MediCare → Add → New Item → search "gitignore" → select it

Or manually create a file called .gitignore at the root of your solution (same level as MediCare.sln) and paste this:

gitignore
# ── Build output ─────────────────────────────────
bin/
obj/

# ── Visual Studio files ───────────────────────────
.vs/
*.user
*.suo
*.userosscache
*.sln.docstates
*.rsuser

# ── ASP.NET Core ──────────────────────────────────
appsettings.Development.json

# ── Environment & Secrets ─────────────────────────
# NEVER push these — they contain real passwords and keys
appsettings.json
*.env
.env
secrets.json

# ── Rider / JetBrains ─────────────────────────────
.idea/

# ── VS Code ───────────────────────────────────────
.vscode/

# ── OS files ──────────────────────────────────────
.DS_Store
Thumbs.db
desktop.ini

# ── NuGet ─────────────────────────────────────────
*.nupkg
.nuget/
packages/

# ── Logs ──────────────────────────────────────────
*.log
logs/

# ── Test results ──────────────────────────────────
TestResults/
coverage/
Notice appsettings.json is ignored — it contains your database password, JWT secret, and API keys. Never push this to GitHub.

Step 2 — Create appsettings.Example.json
Since appsettings.json is gitignored, other developers (or you on another machine) need to know what it looks like. Create a safe example file with no real values.

Create file: MediCare.API/appsettings.Example.json

json
{
  "ConnectionStrings": {
    "DefaultConnection": "Host=localhost;Port=5432;Database=MediCareDB;Username=postgres;Password=YOUR_PASSWORD_HERE"
  },
  "JwtSettings": {
    "SecretKey": "YOUR_SECRET_KEY_MIN_32_CHARS_HERE",
    "Issuer": "MediCare.API",
    "Audience": "MediCare.Client",
    "ExpiryInDays": 7
  },
  "CloudinarySettings": {
    "CloudName": "YOUR_CLOUD_NAME",
    "ApiKey": "YOUR_API_KEY",
    "ApiSecret": "YOUR_API_SECRET"
  },
  "EmailSettings": {
    "Host": "smtp.gmail.com",
    "Port": 587,
    "Username": "YOUR_EMAIL@gmail.com",
    "Password": "YOUR_APP_PASSWORD",
    "FromName": "MediCare+"
  },
  "Logging": {
    "LogLevel": {
      "Default": "Information",
      "Microsoft.AspNetCore": "Warning"
    }
  },
  "AllowedHosts": "*"
}
This file is pushed to GitHub. It shows the structure without exposing real secrets.

Step 3 — Create a proper README.md
Create file: README.md at the root (same level as MediCare.sln)

markdown
# MediCare+ — Online Pharmacy Backend API

A production-level pharmacy e-commerce backend built with ASP.NET Core 8, 
Entity Framework Core, PostgreSQL, and JWT Authentication.

## Tech Stack

- **Framework**: ASP.NET Core 8 Web API
- **ORM**: Entity Framework Core
- **Database**: PostgreSQL
- **Auth**: JWT Bearer Tokens
- **File Storage**: Cloudinary
- **Email**: MailKit
- **Docs**: Swagger / OpenAPI

## Roles

| Role     | Access                                      |
|----------|---------------------------------------------|
| Admin    | Full system access — products, orders,      |
|          | prescriptions, users, reports, inventory    |
| Customer | Browse, cart, orders, prescription upload   |

## Getting Started

### Prerequisites

- .NET 8 SDK
- PostgreSQL
- Visual Studio 2022 or VS Code

### Setup

1. Clone the repository
git clone https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/MediCare.git


2. Navigate to the API project
cd MediCare/MediCare.API


3. Copy the example settings file and fill in your values
cp appsettings.Example.json appsettings.json


4. Run EF Core migrations
dotnet ef database update


5. Run the project
dotnet run


6. Open Swagger UI
https://localhost:PORT/swagger


## Project Structure
MediCare/
├── MediCare.API/
│   ├── Controllers/       # API endpoints
│   ├── Models/            # EF Core entities
│   ├── DTOs/              # Request & Response models
│   ├── Data/              # DbContext
│   ├── Repositories/      # Data access layer
│   ├── Services/          # Business logic
│   ├── Interfaces/        # Service & Repository contracts
│   ├── Helpers/           # JWT, file upload, constants
│   ├── Middleware/        # Global error handler
│   └── Mappings/          # AutoMapper profiles
└── MediCare.sln


## API Modules

- Auth (Register, Login)
- Products & Catalog
- Categories & Brands
- Cart & Wishlist
- Orders & Checkout
- Prescription Upload & Review
- Payments
- Admin Dashboard
- Notifications
- Inventory & Expiry Management

## Environment Variables

Copy `appsettings.Example.json` to `appsettings.json` and fill in:

- `ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection` — PostgreSQL connection string
- `JwtSettings:SecretKey` — Min 32 character secret key
- `CloudinarySettings` — Your Cloudinary account credentials
- `EmailSettings` — Your SMTP credentials

## License

Private project — all rights reserved.
Step 4 — Create a GitHub Repository
Go to github.com → click "New repository"
Fill in:
   Repository name: MediCare
   Description:     Production pharmacy e-commerce backend — ASP.NET Core 8
   Visibility:      Private  ← very important for a real project
Do NOT check "Add README" — we already have one
Click Create repository
Step 5 — Set up Git locally and push
Open Git Bash or PowerShell inside your MediCare root folder (where MediCare.sln is):

bash
# Initialize git
git init

# Add all files (gitignore will automatically exclude secrets)
git add .

# First commit — professional message
git commit -m "feat: initial project setup with models and constants"

# Connect to your GitHub repo
git remote add origin https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/MediCare.git

# Push to GitHub
git branch -M main
git push -u origin main
Step 6 — Branch strategy for a real project
Never code directly on main. Use this branch structure:

main          ← production ready code only
develop       ← active development branch
feature/xxx   ← each new feature gets its own branch
hotfix/xxx    ← urgent bug fixes
Set this up now:

bash
# Create and switch to develop branch
git checkout -b develop

# Push develop branch to GitHub
git push -u origin develop
From now on — every step we do goes into develop, not main. When a full feature is done and tested, we merge into main.

Step 7 — Workflow for every step going forward
Every time we complete a step, you do this:

bash
# See what changed
git status

# Stage all changes
git add .

# Commit with a clear message
git commit -m "feat: add DbContext and EF Core migrations"

# Push to develop
git push origin develop
Professional commit message format
Use this format for every commit:

Prefix	Use for
feat:	New feature or file
fix:	Bug fix
refactor:	Code improvement, no new feature
docs:	README or comments update
chore:	Config, packages, setup
style:	Formatting only
Examples:

bash
git commit -m "feat: add prescription upload endpoint"
git commit -m "fix: resolve null reference in order service"
git commit -m "chore: add NuGet packages for JWT auth"
git commit -m "refactor: clean up product repository"
Your GitHub repo will look like this after pushing:
MediCare (Private repo)
├── MediCare.API/
│   ├── Controllers/
│   ├── Models/            ← all 20 models ✅
│   ├── Helpers/Constants/ ← all constants ✅
│   └── ...
├── .gitignore             ✅
├── appsettings.Example.json ✅
└── README.md              ✅
appsettings.json — NOT on GitHub ✅ (protected by .gitignore)

Once you've pushed successfully, share that you're done and we go straight into Step 3 — DbContext & EF Core Migrations to connect everything to PostgreSQL! 🚀

This error happens because Visual Studio is locking the .vs folder files. Two fixes:

Fix 1 — Close Visual Studio first

bash
git add .
Fix 2 — If still failing, force skip the .vs folder
Your .gitignore should already have .vs/ in it but Git initialized before it was respected. Run this to tell Git to stop tracking it:

bash
git rm -r --cached .vs
Then try again:

bash
git add .
git commit -m "feat: initial project setup with models and constants"
Also — one thing to fix in your .gitignore
I noticed your solution file is MediCare.slnx not MediCare.sln — that's fine, newer Visual Studio uses .slnx. Make sure your .gitignore has this line:

gitignore
.vs/
If it already has it, the issue is just that Visual Studio is open and locking files. Close VS → run git add . → it will work.

Full correct sequence after closing VS:
bash
git add .
git commit -m "feat: initial project setup with models and constants"
git remote add origin https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/MediCare.git
git branch -M main
git push -u origin main
Then:

bash

✅ Require at least 1 approval       ← skip this if solo project

For a solo project you can skip the approval rule but still block direct pushes. Forces you to always go through a Pull Request — good habit.

Claude responded: Step 3 — DbContext & EF Core Migrations
Step 3 — DbContext & EF Core Migrations
We will connect all 20 models to PostgreSQL and create the actual database tables.

1. Create the DbContext
Right click Data folder → Add → Class → name it AppDbContext.cs

csharp
using MediCare.API.Models;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

namespace MediCare.API.Data
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Main database context for MediCare.
    /// Configures all entity relationships, constraints,
    /// indexes and default values for PostgreSQL.
    /// </summary>
    public class AppDbContext : DbContext
    {
        public AppDbContext(DbContextOptions<AppDbContext> options) : base(options) { }

        // ── DbSets (one per table) ────────────────────────
        public DbSet<User> Users { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Address> Addresses { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Category> Categories { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Brand> Brands { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Product> Products { get; set; }
        public DbSet<ProductImage> ProductImages { get; set; }
        public DbSet<InventoryBatch> InventoryBatches { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Cart> Carts { get; set; }
        public DbSet<CartItem> CartItems { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Order> Orders { get; set; }
        public DbSet<OrderItem> OrderItems { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Payment> Payments { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Prescription> Prescriptions { get; set; }
        public DbSet<PrescriptionItem> PrescriptionItems { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Coupon> Coupons { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Review> Reviews { get; set; }
        public DbSet<WishlistItem> WishlistItems { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Notification> Notifications { get; set; }
        public DbSet<AuditLog> AuditLogs { get; set; }

        protected override void OnModelCreating(ModelBuilder modelBuilder)
        {
            base.OnModelCreating(modelBuilder);

            // ── USER ─────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<User>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(u => u.Id);
                entity.HasIndex(u => u.Email).IsUnique();
                entity.HasIndex(u => u.Phone).IsUnique();
                entity.Property(u => u.Email).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(256);
                entity.Property(u => u.Name).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(u => u.Phone).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(20);
                entity.Property(u => u.PasswordHash).IsRequired();
                entity.Property(u => u.Role).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(20);
            });

            // ── ADDRESS ──────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Address>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(a => a.Id);
                entity.Property(a => a.FullName).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(a => a.Phone).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(20);
                entity.Property(a => a.District).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(100);
                entity.Property(a => a.Area).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(a => a.AddressLine).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(300);

                entity.HasOne(a => a.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.Addresses)
                      .HasForeignKey(a => a.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── CATEGORY ─────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Category>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(c => c.Id);
                entity.HasIndex(c => c.Slug).IsUnique();
                entity.Property(c => c.Name).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(c => c.Slug).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(200);

                // Self-referencing relationship for nested categories
                entity.HasOne(c => c.ParentCategory)
                      .WithMany(c => c.SubCategories)
                      .HasForeignKey(c => c.ParentCategoryId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict)
                      .IsRequired(false);
            });

            // ── BRAND ────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Brand>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(b => b.Id);
                entity.HasIndex(b => b.Slug).IsUnique();
                entity.Property(b => b.Name).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(b => b.Slug).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(200);
            });

            // ── PRODUCT ──────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Product>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(p => p.Id);
                entity.HasIndex(p => p.Slug).IsUnique();
                entity.HasIndex(p => p.SKU).IsUnique();
                entity.Property(p => p.Name).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(300);
                entity.Property(p => p.Slug).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(350);
                entity.Property(p => p.SKU).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(100);
                entity.Property(p => p.Price).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(p => p.DiscountPrice).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");

                entity.HasOne(p => p.Category)
                      .WithMany(c => c.Products)
                      .HasForeignKey(p => p.CategoryId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);

                entity.HasOne(p => p.Brand)
                      .WithMany(b => b.Products)
                      .HasForeignKey(p => p.BrandId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);
            });

            // ── PRODUCT IMAGE ─────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<ProductImage>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(pi => pi.Id);
                entity.Property(pi => pi.ImageUrl).IsRequired();

                entity.HasOne(pi => pi.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.Images)
                      .HasForeignKey(pi => pi.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── INVENTORY BATCH ───────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<InventoryBatch>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(ib => ib.Id);
                entity.Property(ib => ib.BatchNumber).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(100);
                entity.Property(ib => ib.PurchasePrice).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(ib => ib.SellingPrice).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");

                entity.HasOne(ib => ib.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.InventoryBatches)
                      .HasForeignKey(ib => ib.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── CART ─────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Cart>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(c => c.Id);

                // One cart per user
                entity.HasIndex(c => c.UserId).IsUnique();

                entity.HasOne(c => c.User)
                      .WithOne(u => u.Cart)
                      .HasForeignKey<Cart>(c => c.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── CART ITEM ─────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<CartItem>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(ci => ci.Id);

                // One product per cart — no duplicates
                entity.HasIndex(ci => new { ci.CartId, ci.ProductId }).IsUnique();

                entity.HasOne(ci => ci.Cart)
                      .WithMany(c => c.CartItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(ci => ci.CartId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);

                entity.HasOne(ci => ci.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.CartItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(ci => ci.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);
            });

            // ── ORDER ─────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Order>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(o => o.Id);
                entity.Property(o => o.Subtotal).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(o => o.Discount).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(o => o.DeliveryCharge).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(o => o.Total).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(o => o.PaymentMethod).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);
                entity.Property(o => o.PaymentStatus).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);
                entity.Property(o => o.OrderStatus).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);

                entity.HasOne(o => o.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.Orders)
                      .HasForeignKey(o => o.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);

                entity.HasOne(o => o.Address)
                      .WithMany(a => a.Orders)
                      .HasForeignKey(o => o.AddressId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);

                entity.HasOne(o => o.Coupon)
                      .WithMany(c => c.Orders)
                      .HasForeignKey(o => o.CouponId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.SetNull)
                      .IsRequired(false);
            });

            // ── ORDER ITEM ────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<OrderItem>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(oi => oi.Id);
                entity.Property(oi => oi.UnitPrice).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(oi => oi.TotalPrice).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");

                entity.HasOne(oi => oi.Order)
                      .WithMany(o => o.OrderItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(oi => oi.OrderId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);

                entity.HasOne(oi => oi.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.OrderItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(oi => oi.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);

                entity.HasOne(oi => oi.Prescription)
                      .WithMany(p => p.OrderItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(oi => oi.PrescriptionId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.SetNull)
                      .IsRequired(false);
            });

            // ── PAYMENT ───────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Payment>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(p => p.Id);
                entity.Property(p => p.Amount).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(p => p.Method).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);
                entity.Property(p => p.Status).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);

                // One payment per order
                entity.HasOne(p => p.Order)
                      .WithOne(o => o.Payment)
                      .HasForeignKey<Payment>(p => p.OrderId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── PRESCRIPTION ──────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Prescription>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(p => p.Id);
                entity.Property(p => p.PatientName).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(150);
                entity.Property(p => p.FileUrl).IsRequired();
                entity.Property(p => p.Status).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);

                entity.HasOne(p => p.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.Prescriptions)
                      .HasForeignKey(p => p.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);
            });

            // ── PRESCRIPTION ITEM ─────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<PrescriptionItem>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(pi => pi.Id);

                entity.HasOne(pi => pi.Prescription)
                      .WithMany(p => p.PrescriptionItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(pi => pi.PrescriptionId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);

                entity.HasOne(pi => pi.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.PrescriptionItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(pi => pi.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);
            });

            // ── COUPON ────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Coupon>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(c => c.Id);
                entity.HasIndex(c => c.Code).IsUnique();
                entity.Property(c => c.Code).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);
                entity.Property(c => c.Amount).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
                entity.Property(c => c.MinOrderAmount).HasColumnType("decimal(18,2)");
            });

            // ── REVIEW ────────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Review>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(r => r.Id);

                // One review per user per product
                entity.HasIndex(r => new { r.UserId, r.ProductId }).IsUnique();

                entity.HasOne(r => r.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.Reviews)
                      .HasForeignKey(r => r.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Restrict);

                entity.HasOne(r => r.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.Reviews)
                      .HasForeignKey(r => r.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── WISHLIST ITEM ─────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<WishlistItem>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(w => w.Id);

                // One product per user wishlist — no duplicates
                entity.HasIndex(w => new { w.UserId, w.ProductId }).IsUnique();

                entity.HasOne(w => w.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.WishlistItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(w => w.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);

                entity.HasOne(w => w.Product)
                      .WithMany(p => p.WishlistItems)
                      .HasForeignKey(w => w.ProductId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── NOTIFICATION ──────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<Notification>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(n => n.Id);
                entity.Property(n => n.Title).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(200);
                entity.Property(n => n.Message).IsRequired();
                entity.Property(n => n.Type).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(50);

                entity.HasOne(n => n.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.Notifications)
                      .HasForeignKey(n => n.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.Cascade);
            });

            // ── AUDIT LOG ─────────────────────────────────────
            modelBuilder.Entity<AuditLog>(entity =>
            {
                entity.HasKey(a => a.Id);
                entity.Property(a => a.Action).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(100);
                entity.Property(a => a.ObjectType).IsRequired().HasMaxLength(100);

                entity.HasOne(a => a.User)
                      .WithMany(u => u.AuditLogs)
                      .HasForeignKey(a => a.UserId)
                      .OnDelete(DeleteBehavior.SetNull)
                      .IsRequired(false);
            });
        }

        /// <summary>
        /// Automatically sets UpdatedAt timestamp
        /// on every SaveChanges call for modified entities.
        /// </summary>
        public override async Task<int> SaveChangesAsync(
            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)
        {
            foreach (var entry in ChangeTracker.Entries<BaseEntity>())
            {
                if (entry.State == EntityState.Modified)
                {
                    entry.Entity.UpdatedAt = DateTime.UtcNow;
                }
            }
            return await base.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken);
        }
    }
}
2. Register DbContext in Program.cs
Open Program.cs and replace everything with this:

csharp
using MediCare.API.Data;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

// ── Services ─────────────────────────────────────────────

builder.Services.AddControllers();
builder.Services.AddEndpointsApiExplorer();

// ── Database ──────────────────────────────────────────────
builder.Services.AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(options =>
    options.UseNpgsql(
        builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("DefaultConnection"),
        npgsqlOptions => npgsqlOptions.CommandTimeout(60)
    )
);

// ── Swagger ───────────────────────────────────────────────
builder.Services.AddSwaggerGen(c =>
{
    c.SwaggerDoc("v1", new()
    {
        Title       = "MediCare API",
        Version     = "v1",
        Description = "Production pharmacy e-commerce backend API"
    });
});

var app = builder.Build();

// ── Middleware pipeline ────────────────────────────────────
if (app.Environment.IsDevelopment())
{
    app.UseSwagger();
    app.UseSwaggerUI(c =>
    {
        c.SwaggerEndpoint("/swagger/v1/swagger.json", "MediCare API v1");
        c.RoutePrefix = "swagger";
    });
}

app.UseHttpsRedirection();
app.UseAuthorization();
app.MapControllers();

// ── Auto-apply migrations on startup ──────────────────────
using (var scope = app.Services.CreateScope())
{
    var db = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<AppDbContext>();
    db.Database.Migrate();
}

app.Run();
3. Install EF Core Tools
Open Tools → NuGet Package Manager → Package Manager Console and run:

bash
dotnet tool install --global dotnet-ef
This installs the dotnet ef command globally on your machine. You only do this once.

4. Create the first Migration
In Package Manager Console run:

bash
cd MediCare.API
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --output-dir Migrations
This generates a Migrations folder with a C# file describing all your tables and relationships. EF Core reads your models and creates the SQL automatically.

You will see a new folder:

MediCare.API/
└── Migrations/
    ├── 20250101000000_InitialCreate.cs
    └── AppDbContextModelSnapshot.cs
5. Apply Migration — Create the database
bash
dotnet ef database update
This connects to PostgreSQL and runs the migration — creating all 20 tables in your MediCareDB database.

You should see:

Applying migration '20250101000000_InitialCreate'.
Done.
6. Verify in pgAdmin
Open pgAdmin → your server → Databases

You should see MediCareDB with these tables:

Tables
├── Users
├── Addresses
├── Categories
├── Brands
├── Products
├── ProductImages
├── InventoryBatches
├── Carts
├── CartItems
├── Orders
├── OrderItems
├── Payments
├── Prescriptions
├── PrescriptionItems
├── Coupons
├── Reviews
├── WishlistItems
├── Notifications
├── AuditLogs
└── __EFMigrationsHistory  ← EF Core tracking table
7. Build and run to verify
Press Ctrl + Shift + B to build — should succeed.

Then press F5 to run — browser should open at:

https://localhost:PORT/swagger
You will see the Swagger UI — empty for now but confirms everything is wired correctly.

8. Commit and push to develop
bash

git commit -m "feat: add AppDbContext with all relationships and initial migration"
git push origin develop
Step 3 done ✅
Your database is now fully created in PostgreSQL with all 20 tables, proper relationships, indexes, constraints and decimal precision — all from your C# models.

What EF Core did for you automatically:

Created all 20 tables
Set up all foreign keys
Created unique indexes on Email, Phone, Slug, SKU, Coupon Code
Set cascade delete rules correctly
Set UpdatedAt to auto-update on every save
Say "Step 3 done" and we move to Step 4 — DTOs and AutoMapper where we define exactly what data goes in and out of every API endpoint! 🚀

Claude responded: The error says "No DbContext was found" — this means EF Core can't find your AppDbContext.
The error says "No DbContext was found" — this means EF Core can't find your AppDbContext. This is a namespace or registration issue. Let's fix it step by step.

Fix 1 — Check your AppDbContext.cs
Open Data/AppDbContext.cs and make sure the namespace is exactly this:

csharp
namespace MediCare.API.Data
And the class looks like this at the top:

csharp
public class AppDbContext : DbContext
Make sure it is not abstract and has no typos.

Fix 2 — Check your Program.cs
Open Program.cs and make sure these two lines exist:

csharp
using MediCare.API.Data;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
And this block is there:

csharp
builder.Services.AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(options =>
    options.UseNpgsql(
        builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("DefaultConnection")
    )
);
Fix 3 — Check appsettings.json exists and has the connection string
Open appsettings.json and confirm this exists:

json
"ConnectionStrings": {
    "DefaultConnection": "Host=localhost;Port=5432;Database=MediCareDB;Username=postgres;Password=YOUR_PASSWORD"
}
Fix 4 — Run migration from the correct location
The Package Manager Console runs differently than a normal terminal. Instead of using it, let's use the regular terminal/PowerShell directly.

Close the Package Manager Console. Open a normal PowerShell or Command Prompt and navigate to your project:

bash
cd E:\MediCare\MediCare\MediCare.API
Then run:

bash
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --output-dir Migrations
Fix 5 — If still failing, specify the project explicitly
Sometimes EF Core needs to be told exactly which project contains the DbContext. Run this:

bash
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --output-dir Migrations --project MediCare.API.csproj --startup-project MediCare.API.csproj
Fix 6 — Check .csproj has EF Core Design package
Open MediCare.API.csproj — double click it in Solution Explorer. Make sure this line exists inside <ItemGroup>:

xml
<PackageReference Include="Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design" Version="*" />
If it is missing, install it via NuGet Package Manager:

Search: Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design → Install
Fix 7 — Full build before migration
In the normal terminal:

bash
cd E:\MediCare\MediCare\MediCare.API
dotnet build
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --output-dir Migrations

is this can be the reason  public AppDbContext(DbContextOptions<AppDbContext> options) : base(options) { } in AppDbContext it is saying to use primary constructor

Mar 29
Claude responded: Yes!
Yes! That is exactly the reason. Visual Studio is suggesting to use a primary constructor which is a C# 12 feature. Since you are on .NET 10, let's use it properly.

Fix — Update AppDbContext.cs constructor
Replace this old style:

csharp
public class AppDbContext : DbContext
{
    public AppDbContext(DbContextOptions<AppDbContext> options) : base(options) { }
    
    // rest of code...
}
With the new primary constructor style:

csharp
public class AppDbContext(DbContextOptions<AppDbContext> options) : DbContext(options)
{
    // rest of code...
}
So your full AppDbContext.cs top should look like this:
csharp
using MediCare.API.Models;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

namespace MediCare.API.Data
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Main database context for MediCare.
    /// Configures all entity relationships, constraints,
    /// indexes and default values for PostgreSQL.
    /// </summary>
    public class AppDbContext(DbContextOptions<AppDbContext> options) : DbContext(options)
    {
        // ── DbSets (one per table) ────────────────────────
        public DbSet<User> Users { get; set; }
        public DbSet<Address> Addresses { get; set; }
        // ... rest of your DbSets and OnModelCreating
    }
}
Then try the migration again
In a normal PowerShell or Command Prompt — not Package Manager Console:

bash
cd E:\MediCare\MediCare\MediCare.API
dotnet build
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate --output-dir Migrations
Why this happens
Old style (C# 9 and below)	New style (C# 12 — .NET 8+)
Constructor defined inside {}	Constructor defined on the class declaration line
More lines of code	Cleaner and shorter
Both work fine	Visual Studio now prefers this
Both styles work — but since you are on .NET 10 with C# 12+, the primary constructor is the modern production way to write it.

Try the build and migration and let me know the result! 🚀

Claude responded: Yes absolutely!
Yes absolutely! You are thinking correctly. We can skip EF Migrations completely and just run the SQL directly in pgAdmin. This is actually a very valid production approach.

Run this in PowerShell first — update .csproj to remove the mapping library for now:
bash
Set-Content -Path "E:\MediCare\MediCare\MediCare.API\MediCare.API.csproj" -Value @'
<Project Sdk="Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Web">

  <PropertyGroup>
    <TargetFramework>net10.0</TargetFramework>
    <Nullable>enable</Nullable>
    <ImplicitUsings>enable</ImplicitUsings>
  </PropertyGroup>

  <ItemGroup>
    <PackageReference Include="Mapster" Version="10.0.0" />
    <PackageReference Include="Mapster.DependencyInjection" Version="10.0.0" />
    <PackageReference Include="BCrypt.Net-Next" Version="4.0.3" />
    <PackageReference Include="CloudinaryDotNet" Version="1.28.0" />
    <PackageReference Include="MailKit" Version="4.15.1" />
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer" Version="10.0.5" />
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore" Version="10.0.5" />
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design" Version="10.0.5">
      <PrivateAssets>all</PrivateAssets>
      <IncludeAssets>runtime; build; native; contentfiles; analyzers; buildtransitive</IncludeAssets>
    </PackageReference>
    <PackageReference Include="Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL" Version="10.0.1" />
    <PackageReference Include="Swashbuckle.AspNetCore" Version="10.1.7" />
    <PackageReference Include="System.IdentityModel.Tokens.Jwt" Version="8.17.0" />
  </ItemGroup>

</Project>
'@
Then:

bash
